> DEVELOPER

Search the desloppify codebase for metadata, descriptions, and user-facing text that might be stale or inaccurate given that the tool has evolved from a "linter/static-analysis tool" to an "agent harness to make slop code well-engineered and beautiful." The current version is 0.9.0.

Check these specific areas and report any issues:

1. README.md — does it exist? What does it say? Is it accurate?
2. CLI help text — look in cli.py and parser.py for description strings
3. docs/ folder — check CLAUDE.md, SKILL.md, and other files for stale descriptions
4. Any hardcoded version strings (search for "0.8" or older version references)
5. CHANGELOG or HISTORY files
6. setup.cfg or any other packaging metadata files
7. The `__init__.py` at the package root — does it have a docstring or version?
8. Any "about" or "description" strings in the codebase that reference old framing like "scanner", "linter", "static analysis tool"
9. License file — does it exist and is it correct?
10. .github/ folder — any workflow descriptions or issue templates with stale text

Report each finding with the file path, line number, current text, and what's wrong with it. Focus on things that are genuinely stale/wrong, not minor style preferences.

tool_result Read

1→# Desloppify - an agent harness to make your codebase 🤌
     2→
     3→[![PyPI version](https://img.shields.io/pypi/v/desloppify)](https://pypi.org/project/desloppify/) ![Python 3.11+](https://img.shields.io/badge/python-3.11%2B-blue)
     4→
     5→Desloppify gives your AI coding agent the tools to identify, understand, and systematically improve codebase quality. It combines mechanical detection (dead code, duplication, complexity) with subjective LLM review (naming, abstractions, module boundaries), then works through a prioritized fix loop. State persists across scans so it chips away over multiple sessions, and the scoring is designed to resist gaming.
     6→
     7→<img src="assets/explained.png" width="100%">
     8→
     9→The score gives your agent a north-star, and the tooling helps it plan, execute, and resolve issues until it hits your target — with a lot of tricks to keep it on track. A score above 98 should correlate with a codebase a seasoned engineer would call beautiful.
    10→
    11→That score generates a scorecard badge for your GitHub profile or README:
    12→
    13→<img src="assets/scorecard.png" width="100%">
    14→
    15→Currently supports 28 languages — full plugin depth for TypeScript, Python, C#, Dart, GDScript, and Go; generic linter + tree-sitter support for Rust, Ruby, Java, Kotlin, and 17 more.
    16→
    17→## For your agent's consideration...
    18→
    19→Paste this prompt into your agent:
    20→
    21→```
    22→I want you to improve the quality of this codebase. To do this, install and run desloppify.
    23→Run ALL of the following (requires Python 3.11+):
    24→
    25→pip install --upgrade "desloppify[full]"
    26→desloppify update-skill claude    # installs the full workflow guide — pick yours: claude, cursor, codex, copilot, windsurf, gemini
    27→
    28→Before scanning, check for directories that should be excluded (vendor, build output,
    29→generated code, worktrees, etc.) and exclude obvious ones with `desloppify exclude <path>`.
    30→Share any questionable candidates with me before excluding.
    31→
    32→desloppify scan --path .
    33→desloppify next
    34→
    35→--path is the directory to scan (use "." for the whole project, or "src/" etc).
    36→
    37→Your goal is to get the strict score as high as possible. The scoring resists gaming — the
    38→only way to improve it is to actually make the code better.
    39→
    40→THE LOOP: run `next`. It tells you what to fix, which file, and the resolve command to run
    41→when done. Fix it, resolve it, run `next` again. Over and over. This is your main job.
    42→
    43→Don't be lazy. Large refactors and small detailed fixes — do both with equal energy. No task
    44→is too big or too small. Fix things properly, not minimally.
    45→
    46→Use `plan` to reorder priorities or cluster related issues. Rescan periodically. The scan
    47→output includes agent instructions — follow them, don't substitute your own analysis.
    48→```
    49→
    50→## From Vibe Coding to Vibe Engineering
    51→
    52→Vibe coding gets things built fast. But the codebases it produces tend to rot in ways that are hard to see and harder to fix — not just the mechanical stuff like dead imports, but the structural kind. Abstractions that made sense at first stop making sense. Naming drifts. Error handling is done three different ways. The codebase works, but working in it gets worse over time.
    53→
    54→LLMs are actually good at spotting this now, if you ask them the right questions. That's the core bet here — that an agent with the right framework can hold a codebase to a real standard, the kind that used to require a senior engineer paying close attention over months.
    55→
    56→So we're trying to define what "good" looks like as a score that's actually worth optimizing. Not a lint score you game to 100 by suppressing warnings. Something where improving the number means the codebase genuinely got better. That's hard, and we're not done, but the anti-gaming stuff matters to us a lot — it's the difference between a useful signal and a vanity metric.
    57→
    58→The hope is that anyone can use this to build something a seasoned engineer would look at and respect. That's the bar we're aiming for.
    59→
    60→If you'd like to join a community of vibe engineers who want to build beautiful things, [come hang out](https://discord.gg/aZdzbZrHaY).
    61→
    62→<img src="assets/engineering.png" width="100%">
    63→
    64→---
    65→
    66→Issues, improvements, and PRs are hugely appreciated — [github.com/peteromallet/desloppify](https://github.com/peteromallet/desloppify).
    67→
    68→MIT License
    69→

1→"""CLI entry point: parse args, load shared context, dispatch command handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import sys
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify.app.cli_support.parser import create_parser as _create_parser
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.registry import get_command_handlers
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import LangResolutionError, resolve_lang
    12→from desloppify.core.exception_sets import CommandError
    13→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import CommandRuntime
    14→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
    15→from desloppify.core.registry import detector_names, on_detector_registered
    16→from desloppify.core.config import load_config
    17→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import set_exclusions
    18→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    19→from desloppify.core.paths_api import get_default_path, get_project_root
    20→from desloppify.core.runtime_state import runtime_scope
    21→from desloppify.languages import available_langs
    22→from desloppify.state import load_state
    23→
    24→_DETECTOR_NAMES: list[str] | None = None
    25→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    26→# Backward-compatible test patch hook; runtime path resolution uses get_project_root().
    27→PROJECT_ROOT = get_project_root()
    28→_PROJECT_ROOT_SENTINEL = PROJECT_ROOT
    29→
    30→
    31→def _get_detector_names() -> list[str]:
    32→    """Return cached detector names, computing on first access."""
    33→    global _DETECTOR_NAMES
    34→    if _DETECTOR_NAMES is None:
    35→        _DETECTOR_NAMES = detector_names()
    36→    return _DETECTOR_NAMES
    37→
    38→
    39→def _invalidate_detector_names_cache() -> None:
    40→    """Invalidate detector-name cache when runtime registrations change."""
    41→    global _DETECTOR_NAMES
    42→    _DETECTOR_NAMES = None
    43→
    44→
    45→on_detector_registered(_invalidate_detector_names_cache)
    46→
    47→def create_parser():
    48→    """Return the top-level argparse parser."""
    49→    return _create_parser(langs=available_langs(), detector_names=_get_detector_names())
    50→
    51→
    52→def _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config: dict):
    53→    """Merge CLI --exclude with persisted config.exclude and apply globally."""
    54→    cli_exclusions = getattr(args, "exclude", None) or []
    55→    persisted = config.get("exclude", [])
    56→    combined = list(cli_exclusions) + [e for e in persisted if e not in cli_exclusions]
    57→    if not combined:
    58→        return
    59→    set_exclusions(combined)
    60→    if cli_exclusions:
    61→        print(
    62→            colorize(f"  Excluding: {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"),
    63→            file=sys.stderr,
    64→        )
    65→        return
    66→    print(
    67→        colorize(
    68→            f"  Excluding (from config): {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"
    69→        ),
    70→        file=sys.stderr,
    71→    )
    72→
    73→
    74→def _resolve_default_path(args) -> None:
    75→    """Fill args.path from detected language or default source path.
    76→
    77→    For the review command, the last scan path (stored in state) is used as the
    78→    default so that ``desloppify review --prepare`` works on the same scope as
    79→    the preceding scan even when the project files are not under ``src/``.
    80→    """
    81→    if getattr(args, "path", None) is not None:
    82→        return
    83→    runtime_root = (
    84→        PROJECT_ROOT.resolve()
    85→        if PROJECT_ROOT is not _PROJECT_ROOT_SENTINEL and isinstance(PROJECT_ROOT, Path)
    86→        else get_project_root()
    87→    )
    88→    if getattr(args, "command", None) == "review":
    89→        try:
    90→            state_file = state_path(args)
    91→            if state_file:
    92→                saved = load_state(state_file)
    93→                saved_path = saved.get("scan_path")
    94→                if saved_path:
    95→                    args.path = str((runtime_root / saved_path).resolve())
    96→                    return
    97→        except (OSError, KeyError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as exc:
    98→            logger.debug("Failed to resolve default path from saved state: %s", exc)
    99→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
   100→    if lang:
   101→        args.path = str(runtime_root / lang.default_src)
   102→    else:
   103→        args.path = str(get_default_path())
   104→
   105→
   106→def _load_shared_runtime(args) -> None:
   107→    """Load config/state and attach shared objects to parsed args."""
   108→    config = load_config()
   109→
   110→    state_file = state_path(args)
   111→    state = load_state(state_file)
   112→    _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config)
   113→
   114→    args.runtime = CommandRuntime(config=config, state=state, state_path=state_file)
   115→
   116→
   117→def _resolve_handler(command: str):
   118→    return get_command_handlers()[command]
   119→
   120→
   121→def _handle_help_command(args, parser) -> None:
   122→    """Handle explicit help command when present in parser config."""
   123→    topic = list(getattr(args, "topic", []) or [])
   124→    try:
   125→        parser.parse_args([*topic, "--help"])
   126→    except SystemExit:
   127→        return
   128→
   129→
   130→def main() -> None:
   131→    # Ensure Unicode output works on Windows terminals (cp1252 etc.)
   132→    for stream in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr):
   133→        if hasattr(stream, "reconfigure"):
   134→            try:
   135→                stream.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
   136→            except (AttributeError, OSError):
   137→                logger.debug(
   138→                    "Skipping stream reconfigure for %s (not supported)",
   139→                    getattr(stream, "name", "<stream>"),
   140→                )
   141→
   142→    parser = create_parser()
   143→    args = parser.parse_args()
   144→    if args.command == "help":
   145→        _handle_help_command(args, parser)
   146→        return
   147→
   148→    try:
   149→        with runtime_scope():
   150→            _resolve_default_path(args)

    29→USAGE_EXAMPLES = """
    30→workflow:
    31→  scan       Run detectors, update state, show diff
    32→  status     Score dashboard with dimension health
    33→  next       Show next highest-priority item to work on
    34→  plan       Living plan: prioritize, cluster, resolve, skip, annotate
    35→
    36→investigate:
    37→  show       Dig into findings by file/dir/detector/ID
    38→  tree       Annotated codebase tree (zoom with --focus)
    39→  viz        Interactive HTML treemap
    40→  detect     Run a single detector directly (bypass state)
    41→
    42→improve:
    43→  autofix    Auto-fix mechanical issues
    44→  suppress   Suppress findings matching a pattern
    45→  exclude    Exclude path pattern from scanning
    46→  move       Move file/dir and update import references
    47→  review     Holistic subjective review (LLM-based)
    48→
    49→configure:
    50→  zone       Show/set zone classifications
    51→  config     Project configuration
    52→  langs      List language plugins
    53→  dev        Developer utilities
    54→  update-skill  Install/update agent skill document
    55→
    56→examples:
    57→  desloppify scan
    58→  desloppify scan --skip-slow --profile ci
    59→  desloppify plan                        # full prioritized markdown
    60→  desloppify plan queue                  # compact table of all items
    61→  desloppify next --count 10             # top 10 queue items
    62→  desloppify show src/components/Modal.tsx
    63→  desloppify plan resolve "unused::src/foo.tsx::React" \\
    64→    --note "removed import" --attest "I have actually ..."
    65→  desloppify review --run-batches --parallel --scan-after-import
    66→"""
    67→
    68→
    69→class _NoAbbrevArgumentParser(argparse.ArgumentParser):
    70→    """Argparse parser variant that disables long-option abbreviation."""
    71→
    72→    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
    73→        kwargs.setdefault("allow_abbrev", False)
    74→        super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
    75→
    76→
    77→def _cli_version_string() -> str:
    78→    """Return the best available CLI version label."""
    79→    try:
    80→        return f"desloppify {get_version('desloppify')}"
    81→    except PackageNotFoundError:
    82→        return "desloppify (version unknown)"
    83→
    84→
    85→def create_parser(*, langs: list[str], detector_names: list[str]) -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
    86→    """Build top-level CLI parser with all subcommands."""
    87→    lang_help = ", ".join(langs) if langs else "registered languages"
    88→
    89→    parser = _NoAbbrevArgumentParser(
    90→        prog="desloppify",
    91→        description="Desloppify — codebase health tracker",
    92→        epilog=USAGE_EXAMPLES,
    93→        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
    94→    )
    95→    parser.add_argument(
    96→        "--lang",
    97→        type=str,
    98→        default=None,
    99→        help=f"Language to scan ({lang_help}). Auto-detected if omitted.",
   100→    )
   101→    parser.add_argument(
   102→        "--exclude",
   103→        action="append",
   104→        default=None,
   105→        metavar="PATTERN",
   106→        help="Path pattern to exclude (component/prefix match; repeatable)",
   107→    )
   108→    parser.add_argument(
   109→        "--version",
   110→        "-V",
   111→        action="version",
   112→        version=_cli_version_string(),
   113→    )
   114→    sub = parser.add_subparsers(
   115→        dest="command",
   116→        required=True,
   117→        parser_class=_NoAbbrevArgumentParser,
   118→    )
   119→    # workflow
   120→    _add_scan_parser(sub)
   121→    _add_status_parser(sub)
   122→    _add_next_parser(sub)
   123→    _add_plan_parser(sub)
   124→    # investigate
   125→    _add_show_parser(sub)
   126→    _add_tree_parser(sub)
   127→    _add_viz_parser(sub)
   128→    _add_detect_parser(sub, detector_names)
   129→    # improve
   130→    _add_fix_parser(sub, langs)
   131→    _add_suppress_parser(sub)
   132→    _add_exclude_parser(sub)
   133→    _add_move_parser(sub)
   134→    _add_review_parser(sub)
   135→    # configure
   136→    _add_zone_parser(sub)
   137→    _add_config_parser(sub)
   138→    _add_langs_parser(sub)
   139→    _add_dev_parser(sub)
   140→    _add_update_skill_parser(sub)
   141→    return parser
   142→
   143→
   144→__all__ = ["USAGE_EXAMPLES", "create_parser"]
   145→

1→<!-- desloppify-begin -->
     2→<!-- desloppify-skill-version: 3 -->
     3→---
     4→name: desloppify
     5→description: >
     6→  Codebase health scanner and technical debt tracker. Use when the user asks
     7→  about code quality, technical debt, dead code, large files, god classes,
     8→  duplicate functions, code smells, naming issues, import cycles, or coupling
     9→  problems. Also use when asked for a health score, what to fix next, or to
    10→  create a cleanup plan. Supports 28 languages.
    11→allowed-tools: Bash(desloppify *)
    12→---
    13→
    14→# Desloppify
    15→
    16→## 1. Your Job
    17→
    18→Maximise the **strict score** honestly. Your main cycle: **scan → plan → execute → rescan**. Follow the scan output's **INSTRUCTIONS FOR AGENTS** — don't substitute your own analysis.
    19→
    20→**Don't be lazy.** Do large refactors and small detailed fixes with equal energy. If it takes touching 20 files, touch 20 files. If it's a one-line change, make it. No task is too big or too small — fix things properly, not minimally.
    21→
    22→## 2. The Workflow
    23→
    24→Three phases, repeated as a cycle.
    25→
    26→### Phase 1: Scan and review — understand the codebase
    27→
    28→```bash
    29→desloppify scan --path .       # analyse the codebase
    30→desloppify status              # check scores — are we at target?
    31→```
    32→
    33→The scan will tell you if subjective dimensions need review. Follow its instructions. To trigger a review manually:
    34→```bash
    35→desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import
    36→```
    37→
    38→### Phase 2: Plan — decide what to work on
    39→
    40→After reviews, triage stages and plan creation appear as queue items in `next`. Complete them in order:
    41→```bash
    42→desloppify next                                        # shows the next workflow step
    43→desloppify plan triage --stage observe --report "themes and root causes..."
    44→desloppify plan triage --stage reflect --report "comparison against completed work..."
    45→desloppify plan triage --stage organize --report "summary of priorities..."
    46→desloppify plan triage --complete --strategy "execution plan..."
    47→```
    48→
    49→Then shape the queue. **The plan shapes everything `next` gives you** — don't skip this step.
    50→
    51→```bash
    52→desloppify plan                          # see the full ordered queue
    53→desloppify plan reorder <pat> top        # reorder — what unblocks the most?
    54→desloppify plan cluster create <name>    # group related issues to batch-fix
    55→desloppify plan focus <cluster>          # scope next to one cluster
    56→desloppify plan skip <pat>              # defer — hide from next
    57→```
    58→
    59→More plan commands:
    60→```bash
    61→desloppify plan reorder <cluster> top    # move all cluster members at once
    62→desloppify plan reorder <a> <b> top     # mix clusters + findings in one reorder
    63→desloppify plan reorder <pat> before -t X  # position relative to another item/cluster
    64→desloppify plan cluster reorder a,b top # reorder multiple clusters as one block
    65→desloppify plan resolve <pat>           # mark complete
    66→desloppify plan reopen <pat>             # reopen
    67→```
    68→
    69→### Phase 3: Execute — grind the queue to completion
    70→
    71→Trust the plan and execute. Don't rescan mid-queue — finish the queue first.
    72→
    73→**Branch first.** Create a dedicated branch for health work — never commit directly to main:
    74→```bash
    75→git checkout -b desloppify/code-health    # or desloppify/<focus-area>
    76→```
    77→
    78→**Set up commit tracking.** If you have a PR, link it for auto-updated descriptions:
    79→```bash
    80→desloppify config set commit_pr 42        # PR number for auto-updates
    81→```
    82→
    83→**The loop:**
    84→```
    85→1. desloppify next              ← what to fix next
    86→2. Fix the issue in code
    87→3. Resolve it (next shows you the exact command including required attestation)
    88→4. When you have a logical batch, commit:
    89→   git add <files> && git commit -m "desloppify: fix 3 deferred_import findings"
    90→5. Record the commit:
    91→   desloppify plan commit-log record      # moves findings uncommitted → committed, updates PR
    92→6. Push periodically:
    93→   git push -u origin desloppify/code-health
    94→7. Repeat until the queue is empty
    95→```
    96→
    97→Score may temporarily drop after fixes — cascade effects are normal, keep going.
    98→If `next` suggests an auto-fixer, run `desloppify autofix <fixer> --dry-run` to preview, then apply.
    99→
   100→**When the queue is clear, go back to Phase 1.** New issues will surface, cascades will have resolved, priorities will have shifted. This is the cycle.
   101→
   102→### Other useful commands
   103→
   104→```bash
   105→desloppify next --count 5                         # top 5 priorities
   106→desloppify next --cluster <name>                  # drill into a cluster
   107→desloppify show <pattern>                         # filter by file/detector/ID
   108→desloppify show --status open                     # all open findings
   109→desloppify plan skip --permanent "<id>" --note "reason" --attest "..." # accept debt
   110→desloppify exclude <path>                         # exclude a directory from scanning
   111→desloppify config show                            # show all config including excludes
   112→desloppify scan --path . --reset-subjective       # reset subjective baseline to 0
   113→```
   114→
   115→## 3. Reference
   116→
   117→### How scoring works
   118→
   119→Overall score = **40% mechanical** + **60% subjective**.
   120→
   121→- **Mechanical (40%)**: auto-detected issues — duplication, dead code, smells, unused imports, security. Fixed by changing code and rescanning.
   122→- **Subjective (60%)**: design quality review — naming, error handling, abstractions, clarity. Starts at **0%** until reviewed. The scan will prompt you when a review is needed.
   123→- **Strict score** is the north star: wontfix items count as open. The gap between overall and strict is your wontfix debt.
   124→- **Score types**: overall (lenient), strict (wontfix counts), objective (mechanical only), verified (confirmed fixes only).
   125→
   126→### Subjective reviews in detail
   127→
   128→- **Preferred**: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` — does everything in one command.
   129→- **Manual path**: `desloppify review --prepare` → review per dimension → `desloppify review --import file.json`.
   130→- Import first, fix after — import creates tracked state entries for correlation.
   131→- Target-matching scores trigger auto-reset to prevent gaming.
   132→- Even moderate scores (60-80) dramatically improve overall health.
   133→- Stale dimensions auto-surface in `next` — just follow the queue.
   134→
   135→### Review output format
   136→
   137→Return machine-readable JSON for review imports. For `--external-submit`, include `session` from the generated template:
   138→
   139→```json
   140→{
   141→  "session": {
   142→    "id": "<session_id_from_template>",
   143→    "token": "<session_token_from_template>"
   144→  },
   145→  "assessments": {
   146→    "<dimension_from_query>": 0
   147→  },
   148→  "findings": [
   149→    {
   150→      "dimension": "<dimension_from_query>",
   151→      "identifier": "short_id",
   152→      "summary": "one-line defect summary",
   153→      "related_files": ["relative/path/to/file.py"],
   154→      "evidence": ["specific code observation"],
   155→      "suggestion": "concrete fix recommendation",
   156→      "confidence": "high|medium|low"
   157→    }
   158→  ]
   159→}
   160→```
   161→
   162→**Import rules:**
   163→- `findings` MUST match `query.system_prompt` exactly (including `related_files`, `evidence`, and `suggestion`). Use `"findings": []` when no defects found.
   164→- Import is fail-closed: invalid findings abort unless `--allow-partial` is passed.
   165→- Assessment scores are auto-applied from trusted internal or cloud session imports. Legacy `--attested-external` remains supported.
   166→
   167→**Import paths:**
   168→- Robust session flow (recommended): `desloppify review --external-start --external-runner claude` → use generated prompt/template → run printed `--external-submit` command.
   169→- Durable scored import (legacy): `desloppify review --import findings.json --attested-external --attest "I validated this review was completed without awareness of overall score and is unbiased."`
   170→- Findings-only fallback: `desloppify review --import findings.json`
   171→
   172→### Review integrity
   173→
   174→1. Do not use prior chat context, score history, or target-threshold anchoring.
   175→2. Score from evidence only; when mixed, score lower and explain uncertainty.
   176→3. Assess every requested dimension; never drop one. If evidence is weak, score lower.
   177→
   178→### Reviewer agent prompt
   179→
   180→Runners that support agent definitions (Cursor, Copilot, Gemini) can create a dedicated reviewer agent. Use this system prompt:
   181→
   182→```
   183→You are a code quality reviewer. You will be given a codebase path, a set of
   184→dimensions to score, and what each dimension means. Read the code, score each
   185→dimension 0-100 from evidence only, and return JSON in the required format.
   186→Do not anchor to target thresholds. When evidence is mixed, score lower and
   187→explain uncertainty.
   188→```
   189→
   190→See your editor's overlay section below for the agent config format.
   191→
   192→### Commit tracking & branch workflow
   193→
   194→Work on a dedicated branch named `desloppify/<description>` (e.g., `desloppify/code-health`, `desloppify/fix-smells`). Never push health work directly to main.
   195→
   196→```bash
   197→desloppify config set commit_pr 42              # link to your PR
   198→desloppify plan commit-log                      # see uncommitted + committed status
   199→desloppify plan commit-log record               # record HEAD commit, update PR description
   200→desloppify plan commit-log record --note "why"  # with rationale
```

```
1→## Claude Code Overlay
     2→
     3→Use Claude subagents for subjective scoring work. **Do not use `--runner codex`** — use Claude subagents exclusively.
     4→
     5→### Review workflow
     6→
     7→Run `desloppify review --prepare` first to generate review data, then use Claude subagents:
     8→
     9→1. **Prepare**: `desloppify review --prepare` — writes `query.json` and `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json`.
    10→2. **Launch subagents**: Split the review across N parallel Claude subagents (one message, multiple Task calls). Each agent reviews a subset of dimensions.
    11→3. **Merge & import**: Merge agent outputs, then `desloppify review --import merged.json --manual-override --attest "Claude subagents ran blind reviews against review_packet_blind.json" --scan-after-import`.
    12→
    13→#### How to split dimensions across subagents
    14→
    15→- Read `dimension_prompts` from `query.json` for dimensions with definitions and seed files.
    16→- Read `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` for the blind packet (no score targets, no anchoring data).
    17→- Group dimensions into 3-4 batches by theme (e.g., architecture, code quality, testing, conventions).
    18→- Launch one Task agent per batch with `subagent_type: "general-purpose"`. Each agent gets:
    19→  - The codebase path and list of dimensions to score
    20→  - The blind packet path to read
    21→  - Instruction to score from code evidence only, not from targets
    22→- Each agent writes output to a separate file. Merge assessments (average overlapping dimension scores) and concatenate findings.
    23→
    24→### Subagent rules
    25→
    26→1. Each agent must be context-isolated — do not pass conversation history or score targets.
    27→2. Agents must consume `.desloppify/review_packet_blind.json` (not full `query.json`) to avoid score anchoring.
    28→
    29→<!-- desloppify-overlay: claude -->
    30→<!-- desloppify-end -->
    31→
```

1→## Codex Overlay
     2→
     3→This is the canonical Codex overlay used by the README install command.
     4→
     5→1. Prefer first-class batch runs: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.
     6→2. The command writes immutable packet snapshots under `.desloppify/review_packets/holistic_packet_*.json`; use those for reproducible retries.
     7→3. Keep reviewer input scoped to the immutable packet and the source files named in each batch.
     8→4. If a batch fails, retry only that slice with `desloppify review --run-batches --packet <packet.json> --only-batches <idxs>`.
     9→5. Manual override is safety-scoped: you cannot combine it with `--allow-partial`, and provisional manual scores expire on the next `scan` unless replaced by trusted internal or attested-external imports.

1→# Publish to PyPI from controlled release triggers only.
     2→
     3→name: Publish to PyPI
     4→
     5→on:
     6→  release:
     7→    types: [published]
     8→  push:
     9→    tags:
    10→      - "v*"
    11→  workflow_dispatch:
    12→
    13→permissions:
    14→  contents: read
    15→
    16→concurrency:
    17→  group: publish-pypi-${{ github.ref }}
    18→  cancel-in-progress: false
    19→
    20→jobs:
    21→  publish:
    22→    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    23→    permissions:
    24→      id-token: write  # required for trusted publishing
    25→
    26→    environment:
    27→      name: pypi
    28→      url: https://pypi.org/p/desloppify
    29→
    30→    steps:
    31→      - uses: actions/checkout@v4
    32→
    33→      - uses: actions/setup-python@v5
    34→        with:
    35→          python-version: "3.11"
    36→          cache: pip
    37→          cache-dependency-path: pyproject.toml
    38→
    39→      - name: Get version from pyproject.toml
    40→        id: version
    41→        run: |
    42→          VERSION=$(python -c "import tomllib; print(tomllib.loads(open('pyproject.toml').read())['project']['version'])")
    43→          echo "version=$VERSION" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
    44→
    45→      - name: Validate tag matches package version
    46→        if: startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')
    47→        run: |
    48→          TAG_VERSION="${GITHUB_REF#refs/tags/v}"
    49→          PKG_VERSION="${{ steps.version.outputs.version }}"
    50→          if [ "$TAG_VERSION" != "$PKG_VERSION" ]; then
    51→            echo "Tag version $TAG_VERSION does not match package version $PKG_VERSION"
    52→            exit 1
    53→          fi
    54→
    55→      - name: Check if version exists on PyPI
    56→        id: check
    57→        run: |
    58→          STATUS=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}" "https://pypi.org/pypi/desloppify/${{ steps.version.outputs.version }}/json")
    59→          if [ "$STATUS" = "200" ]; then
    60→            echo "exists=true" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
    61→            echo "Version ${{ steps.version.outputs.version }} already on PyPI — skipping publish."
    62→          else
    63→            echo "exists=false" >> "$GITHUB_OUTPUT"
    64→            echo "Version ${{ steps.version.outputs.version }} not on PyPI — will publish."
    65→          fi
    66→
    67→      - name: Run packaging smoke gate
    68→        if: steps.check.outputs.exists == 'false'
    69→        run: make package-smoke
    70→
    71→      - name: Publish to PyPI
    72→        if: steps.check.outputs.exists == 'false'
    73→        uses: pypa/gh-action-pypi-publish@release/v1
    74→        with:
    75→          packages-dir: dist/
    76→
```

   130→def _fixer_help_lines(langs: list[str]) -> list[str]:
   131→    fixer_help_lines: list[str] = []
   132→    for lang_name in langs:
   133→        try:
   134→            fixer_names = sorted(get_lang(lang_name).fixers.keys())
   135→        except (ImportError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError):
   136→            fixer_names = []
   137→        fixer_list = ", ".join(fixer_names) if fixer_names else "none yet"
   138→        fixer_help_lines.append(f"fixers ({lang_name}): {fixer_list}")
   139→    return fixer_help_lines

"""Detect architectural layer violations via import-linter.

Reads `.importlinter` config from the scanned project's root (walking up
from the scan path to find the first `.importlinter` file, stopping at `.git`
boundaries). Falls back gracefully to None when lint-imports is not installed
or no config is found.

See https://import-linter.readthedocs.io for config format.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import logging
import re
import subprocess
from pathlib import Path

logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)

_BROKEN_CONTRACT_RE = re.compile(r"Broken contract '([^']+)'")

"""Quality-focused Python detector phase runners."""

from __future__ import annotations

from pathlib import Path

from desloppify import state as state_mod
from desloppify.engine.policy.zones import adjust_potential, filter_entries
from desloppify.languages._framework.finding_factories import make_smell_findings
from desloppify.languages._framework.runtime import LangRun
from desloppify.languages.python.detectors import dict_keys as dict_keys_detector_mod
from desloppify.languages.python.detectors import (
    import_linter_adapter as import_linter_adapter_mod,
)
from desloppify.languages.python.detectors import (
    mutable_state as mutable_state_detector_mod,
)
from desloppify.languages.python.detectors import smells as smells_detector_mod
from desloppify.languages.python.detectors.ruff_smells import detect_with_ruff_smells
from desloppify.state import Finding
from desloppify.core.output_api import log


def phase_smells(path: Path, lang: LangRun) -> tuple[list[Finding], dict[str, int]]:
    """Run file/code smell detectors plus cross-file signature variance."""
    entries, total_files = smells_detector_mod.detect_smells(path)
    # Supplement with ruff B/E/W rules not covered by the regex smells above.
    ruff_entries = detect_with_ruff_smells(path)
    if ruff_entries:
        entries = entries + ruff_entries
```

1→# Languages
     2→
     3→Desloppify supports 28 languages through a plugin system with two tiers: **full plugins** (6) with hand-written detectors and subjective review, and **generic plugins** (22) that wrap external linters and optionally use tree-sitter for AST analysis.
     4→
     5→## Full Plugins
     6→
     7→These have custom detectors, language-specific smell analysis, subjective review dimensions, auto-fixers, and deep scoring. They're multi-module packages with the full plugin contract.
     8→
     9→| Language | Path | Key capabilities |
    10→|----------|------|-----------------|
    11→| **Python** | `python/` | AST smell detection, ruff/bandit adapters, import-linter, unused detection, security, auto-fix |
    12→| **TypeScript** | `typescript/` | React-aware detectors, knip adapter, 7 auto-fixers, props/exports/concerns analysis |
    13→| **C#/.NET** | `csharp/` | Structural + coupling + security, dotnet-based dep analysis |
    14→| **Dart** | `dart/` | Flutter-aware, pubspec integration, test coverage mapping |
    15→| **GDScript** | `gdscript/` | Godot scene-aware, tree-sitter phases, shared framework helpers |
    16→| **Go** | `go/` | golangci-lint + go vet adapters, regex function extraction, test coverage mapping |
    17→
    18→Example: `python/` — see `python/__init__.py` for the full `@register_lang()` config with 15+ detector phases, custom extractors, security hooks, and review guidance.
    19→
    20→## Generic Plugins
    21→
    22→These are single-file plugins (~20-40 lines) that call `generic_lang()` with external tool specs. When `tree-sitter-language-pack` is installed, they also get AST-powered analysis for free.
    23→
    24→| Language | Path | Tools | Tree-sitter |
    25→|----------|------|-------|-------------|
    26→| Rust | `rust/` | cargo clippy, cargo check | functions, imports, structs |
    27→| Ruby | `ruby/` | rubocop | functions, methods, classes, imports |
    28→| Java | `java/` | checkstyle | functions, constructors, classes, imports |
    29→| Kotlin | `kotlin/` | ktlint, detekt | functions, classes, imports |
    30→| Swift | `swift/` | swiftlint | functions, classes |
    31→| PHP | `php/` | phpstan | functions, methods, classes, imports |
    32→| Scala | `scala/` | scalac | functions, classes, imports |
    33→| Elixir | `elixir/` | credo | functions, imports |
    34→| Haskell | `haskell/` | hlint | functions, imports |
    35→| JavaScript | `javascript/` | eslint | functions, methods, classes, imports |
    36→| C/C++ | `cxx/` | cppcheck | functions, classes, includes |
    37→| Bash | `bash/` | shellcheck | functions, source imports |
    38→| Lua | `lua/` | luacheck | functions, imports |
    39→| Perl | `perl/` | perlcritic | subroutines, imports |
    40→| Clojure | `clojure/` | clj-kondo | functions |
    41→| Zig | `zig/` | zig build | functions, imports |
    42→| Nim | `nim/` | nim check | functions |
    43→| PowerShell | `powershell/` | PSScriptAnalyzer | functions |
    44→| R | `r/` | — | functions, imports |
    45→| Erlang | `erlang/` | dialyzer | functions, imports |
    46→| OCaml | `ocaml/` | ocaml compiler | functions, modules, imports |
    47→| F# | `fsharp/` | dotnet build | functions, imports |
    48→
    49→Example: `rust/__init__.py` — wraps cargo clippy + cargo check, adds Rust-specific zone rules and test coverage hooks.
    50→
    51→## What Each Tier Gets
    52→
    53→### Every plugin (generic or full)
    54→- Security scanning (cross-language patterns)
    55→- Subjective review (LLM-powered design review)
    56→- Boilerplate + duplicate detection
    57→- Zone classification (test/vendor/config/generated)
    58→- Scoring integration (automatic detector → dimension mapping)
    59→
    60→### Generic plugins with tree-sitter
    61→All of the above, plus:
    62→- Function/method extraction → duplicate detection
    63→- Import parsing → dependency graph → coupling/orphan/cycle detection
    64→- Test coverage analysis (import-based + naming convention mapping)
    65→- AST complexity: nesting depth, cyclomatic complexity, long functions, parameter count, callback depth
    66→- God class/struct detection (methods > 15, LOC > 500, attributes > 10)
    67→- Responsibility cohesion (disconnected function clusters)
    68→- Unused import detection
    69→- Signature variance analysis
    70→- AST smell detection (empty catch, unreachable code)
    71→
    72→### Full plugins additionally get
    73→- Custom per-line smell detectors (language-specific patterns)
    74→- Language-aware auto-fixers
    75→- Custom subjective review dimensions
    76→- Hand-tuned coupling and dependency rules
    77→- Framework-specific detectors (e.g., React hooks, Flutter widgets)
    78→
    79→## The Difference
    80→
    81→A generic Rust plugin runs cargo clippy and gets tree-sitter analysis — that covers most of what you need. A full Python plugin has 15+ hand-written detector phases that understand Python-specific patterns like mutable class variables, `lru_cache` on methods with mutable args, subprocess calls without timeouts, naive regex backtracking, and dozens more. It has auto-fixers that can rewrite imports. It has subjective review dimensions tuned for Python conventions.
    82→
    83→The generic system is the right starting point for any language. Only invest in a full plugin when you need detectors that understand language idioms beyond what AST pattern matching and external linters can catch.
    84→
    85→## Adding a New Generic Plugin
    86→
    87→Create `languages/<name>/__init__.py`:
    88→
    89→```python
    90→from desloppify.languages._framework.generic import generic_lang
    91→from desloppify.languages._framework.treesitter._specs import MY_SPEC  # if available
    92→
    93→generic_lang(
    94→    name="mylang",
    95→    extensions=[".ml"],
    96→    tools=[
    97→        {"label": "mylinter", "cmd": "mylinter --json .",
    98→         "fmt": "json", "id": "mylinter_issue", "tier": 2,
    99→         "fix_cmd": "mylinter --fix ."},
   100→    ],
   101→    exclude=["vendor"],
   102→    detect_markers=["myproject.toml"],
   103→    treesitter_spec=MY_SPEC,
   104→)
   105→```
   106→
   107→That's it. Auto-discovered at startup, zero shared-code edits.
   108→
   109→## Upgrading Generic → Full
   110→
   111→Only needed when you want things generic plugins can't provide: custom per-line smell detectors, language-specific coupling rules, custom fixers with AST rewriting, or full control over phase ordering.
   112→
   113→The path is incremental:
   114→
   115→1. **Start generic** — `generic_lang()` with tool specs + tree-sitter
   116→2. **Extend in-place** — add zone rules, test coverage hooks, security hooks (stays generic)
   117→3. **Go full** — when you need custom detectors or fixers, switch to `@register_lang()` with a package directory
   118→
   119→Bootstrap: `desloppify dev scaffold-lang <name> --extension .ext --marker <root-marker>`
   120→
   121→Required package structure (validated at registration): `__init__.py`, `commands.py`, `extractors.py`, `phases.py`, `move.py`, `review.py`, `test_coverage.py`, plus `detectors/`, `fixers/`, and `tests/` directories.
   122→
   123→A full plugin can still reuse generic building blocks — `_run_tool()` for external tools, tree-sitter extractors, shared phase builders (`detector_phase_security()`, `detector_phase_test_coverage()`, `shared_subjective_duplicates_tail()`).
   124→
   125→## Shared Framework (`_framework/`)
   126→
   127→```
   128→_framework/
   129→├── generic.py           # generic_lang() factory for tool-based plugins
   130→├── base/                # LangConfig, DetectorPhase, FixerConfig contracts
   131→│   ├── types.py         # Core dataclasses
   132→│   ├── shared_phases.py # Shared detector phase runners
   133→│   ├── phase_builders.py # Phase builder helpers
   134→│   └── structural.py   # Structural analysis utilities
   135→├── treesitter/          # Tree-sitter integration (optional)
   136→│   ├── _specs.py        # Per-language tree-sitter query definitions
   137→│   ├── _extractors.py   # Function/class extraction via AST
   138→│   ├── _imports.py      # Import parsing + per-language resolvers
   139→│   ├── _complexity.py   # AST complexity signals
   140→│   ├── _smells.py       # Cross-language AST smell detectors
   141→│   ├── _cohesion.py     # Responsibility cohesion
   142→│   └── _unused_imports.py
   143→├── runtime.py           # LangRun (per-run mutable execution state)
   144→├── resolution.py        # get_lang/available_langs/auto_detect_lang
   145→├── discovery.py         # Plugin auto-discovery
   146→├── commands_base.py     # Shared detect-command factories
   147→└── review_data/         # Shared review dimension JSON payloads
   148→```
   149→
   150→## Design Rules
   151→
   152→- Import direction: `languages/<name>/` → `engine/detectors/` and `languages/_framework/*`. Never the reverse.
   153→- Keep language plugin code in its language folder
   154→- Keep reusable cross-language framework code in `_framework/`
   155→- Generic plugins should NOT mirror the full plugin directory structure — they are intentionally minimal
   156→
   157→## Testing
   158→
   159→```bash
   160→# Run all language tests
   161→pytest -q desloppify/tests/lang/common/ desloppify/languages/*/tests/
   162→
   163→# Run one language's tests
   164→pytest -q desloppify/languages/go/tests/
   165→
   166→# Validate plugin contracts
   167→pytest -q desloppify/tests/lang/common/test_lang_standardization.py
   168→```
   169→
```

1→# Desloppify — Technical Internals
     2→
     3→Traditional tools catch mechanical issues — linters, formatters, dead code finders. Desloppify wraps those but the point is **subjective analysis**: structured LLM prompts about architecture, design quality, and convention consistency, tracked as scored findings. The score weights subjective findings heavily because that's what actually moves the needle. See the top-level README for philosophy and usage.
     4→
     5→## Directory Layout
     6→
     7→```
     8→desloppify/
     9→├── cli.py              # Argparse, main()
    10→├── state.py            # Persistent-state facade
    11→├── utils.py            # File discovery, path helpers
    12→├── hook_registry.py    # Detector-safe language hook registry
    13→├── app/                # CLI layer (commands, parser, output)
    14→├── engine/             # Scan/scoring/state internals
    15→│   ├── detectors/      # Generic algorithms (zero language knowledge)
    16→│   ├── planning/       # Prioritization and plan generation
    17→│   ├── policy/         # Zones, scoring policy
    18→│   ├── _scoring/
    19→│   ├── _state/
    20→│   └── _work_queue/
    21→├── intelligence/       # Subjective/narrative/review layer
    22→│   ├── narrative/
    23→│   ├── integrity/
    24→│   └── review/
    25→└── languages/          # Language plugins (auto-discovered, see languages/README.md)
    26→    ├── _framework/     # Shared plugin framework, generic_lang(), tree-sitter
    27→    ├── python/         # Full plugins (custom detectors, fixers, review dims)
    28→    ├── typescript/
    29→    ├── csharp/, dart/, gdscript/, go/
    30→    └── rust/, ruby/, java/, ... (22 generic plugins)
    31→```
    32→
    33→## Architecture
    34→
    35→```
    36→Layer 1: engine/detectors/       Generic algorithms. Data-in, data-out. Zero language imports.
    37→Layer 2: languages/_framework/   Shared contracts/helpers. Normalize raw results → tiered findings.
    38→Layer 3: languages/<name>/       Language config + phases + extractors + detectors + fixers.
    39→```
    40→
    41→**Import direction**: `languages/` → `engine/detectors/`. Never the reverse. Detectors needing language-specific behavior use `hook_registry.get_lang_hook(...)`.
    42→
    43→## Data Flow
    44→
    45→```
    46→scan:    LangConfig → LangRun(phases) → generate_findings() → merge_scan() → state-{lang}.json
    47→fix:     LangConfig.fixers → fixer.fix() → resolve in state
    48→detect:  LangConfig.detect_commands[name](args) → display
    49→```
    50→
    51→## Contracts
    52→
    53→**Detector**: `detect_*(data, config) → list[dict]` — generic algorithm, no language assumptions.
    54→
    55→**Phase runner**: `_phase_*(path, lang) → (list[Finding], dict[str, int])` — thin orchestrator calling extractors → generic algorithms → normalization.
    56→
    57→**LangConfig**: Static language contract. Owns phases, detectors, thresholds, hooks.
    58→
    59→**LangRun**: Per-invocation runtime wrapper (`_framework/runtime.py`) carrying mutable state (zone_map, dep_graph, complexity_map). Phases execute against LangRun, not LangConfig.
    60→
    61→## Rules
    62→
    63→- Entry command modules stay thin — behavioral logic in delegated modules
    64→- Dynamic imports only in `languages/__init__.py` (discovery) and `hook_registry.py` (hooks)
    65→- Persistent schema owned by `state.py` + `engine/_state/`. Command modules don't introduce ad-hoc persisted fields
    66→- `LangRun` owns per-run mutable state, not `LangConfig`
    67→
    68→## Non-Obvious Behavior
    69→
    70→- **State scoping**: `merge_scan` only auto-resolves findings matching the scan's `lang` and `scan_path`. A Python scan never touches TS state.
    71→- **Suspect guard**: If a detector drops from >=5 findings to 0, disappearances are held (bypass: `--force-resolve`).
    72→- **Scoring**: Weighted by tier (T4=4x, T1=1x). Strict score penalizes both open and wontfix.
    73→- **Cascade effects**: Fixing one category (e.g. unused imports) can surface work for the next (unused vars). Score can temporarily drop.
    74→- **Tree-sitter optional**: All tree-sitter features degrade gracefully. Without `tree-sitter-language-pack`, generic plugins fall back to tool-only mode.
    75→- **Bandit optional for Python depth**: Without `bandit`, Python-specific security checks are skipped; scan surfaces preflight/post-scan coverage warnings and marks score confidence reduced for security.
    76→
```

# Development Philosophy

This is a tool for agents. That shapes everything about how we build it.

## Agent-first

The primary user is an AI coding agent, not a human. The CLI output, the scoring model, the state format — all of it is optimized for agent consumption. Humans interact with it, but when there's a tradeoff between agent effectiveness and human UX, agent wins.

## No compatibility promise

/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/docs/DEVELOPMENT_PHILOSOPHY.md
## Windsurf Overlay

Windsurf does not support spawning subagents from within a Cascade session.
If not using the batch runner, parallel reviews require the user to open
multiple Cascade panes manually.

### Review workflow

1. Run `desloppify review --prepare` to generate `query.json`.
2. Ask the user to open additional Cascade panes for parallel review.
/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/docs/WINDSURF.md
## Cursor Overlay

Cursor supports native subagents via `.cursor/agents/` definitions. Use them
for context-isolated subjective reviews.

### Review workflow

Define a reviewer agent in `.cursor/agents/desloppify-reviewer.md`:

```markdown
/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/docs/CURSOR.md
## Codex Overlay

This is the canonical Codex overlay used by the README install command.

1. Prefer first-class batch runs: `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import`.
2. The command writes immutable packet snapshots under `.desloppify/review_packets/holistic_packet_*.json`; use those for reproducible retries.
3. Keep reviewer input scoped to the immutable packet and the source files named in each batch.
4. If a batch fails, retry only that slice with `desloppify review --run-batches --packet <packet.json> --only-batches <idxs>`.
5. Manual override is safety-scoped: you cannot combine it with `--allow-partial`, and provisional manual scores expire on the next `scan` unless replaced by trusted internal or attested-external imports.

/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/docs/CODEX.md
# CI/CD Plan

This document defines the repository CI/CD operating model and required checks.

## Goals

1. Block merges unless quality gates pass.
2. Decouple package publishing from ordinary pushes.
3. Keep expensive integration checks visible and reproducible.

"""Desloppify — Automated cruft detection + LLM-ready analysis."""

**Issue**: This describes the tool as "Automated cruft detection" which frames it as a scanner/detector tool. The README and actual purpose describe it as "an agent harness to make your codebase well-engineered and beautiful." The docstring should emphasize the agent harness and iterative improvement framing.
**Why it's wrong**: Misleads about the tool's purpose and evolution from static analysis to agent-driven refactoring.

Do a thorough sweep of the desloppify codebase looking for aesthetic warts — small inconsistencies, stale comments, misleading names, dead code, formatting oddities, etc. Focus on things that make the code look unpolished or confusing, NOT on feature requests or architecture changes.

Categories to check:

1. **Stale/misleading comments** — comments that describe behavior that's changed, TODO comments that are done, references to old names
2. **Inconsistent naming** — e.g. some modules use `cmd_` prefix and others don't, mixed snake_case patterns, abbreviations used inconsistently
3. **Dead imports** — imports that are no longer used
4. **Orphaned __all__ entries** — entries in __all__ that reference things not defined in the module, or missing entries for things that are exported
5. **Inconsistent string formatting** — mixed f-strings and .format(), mixed quote styles in the same file where it's jarring
6. **Stale references to old module names** — e.g. if files were renamed but comments/docstrings still reference old names
7. **Duplicate definitions** — constants or helpers defined in multiple places
8. **Type annotation inconsistencies** — e.g. `dict` vs `Dict`, `list` vs `List` mixed in the same file
9. **Empty files or near-empty files** that serve no purpose
10. **Confusing re-exports** — things imported and re-exported through multiple layers unnecessarily

    48→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    49→class QueueBuildOptions:

    52→    ``scan_path`` defaults to reading from ``state["scan_path"]`` so callers

    81→class WorkQueueResult(TypedDict):
    82→    """Typed shape of the dict returned by :func:`build_work_queue`."""
    83→

    90→def build_work_queue(
    91→    state: StateModel,
    92→    *,
    93→    options: QueueBuildOptions | None = None,
    94→) -> WorkQueueResult:
    95→    """Build a ranked work queue from state findings.
    96→
    97→    Pipeline:
    98→    1. Gather — finding items, subjective dimensions, workflow stages
    99→    2. Score  — estimate impact from dimension headroom, apply floor
   100→    3. Order  — stamp plan positions, sort, filter to cluster focus
   101→    4. Limit  — truncate to count, optionally add explain metadata
   102→    """
   103→    opts = options or QueueBuildOptions()
   104→    plan, scan_path, status, threshold = _resolve_inputs(opts, state)
   105→
   106→    # 1. Gather
   107→    items = build_finding_items(
   108→        state, scan_path=scan_path, status_filter=status,
   109→        scope=opts.scope, chronic=opts.chronic,
   110→    )
   111→    items += _gather_subjective_items(state, opts, plan, threshold)
   112→    items += _gather_workflow_items(state, plan, status)
   113→
   114→    # 2. Score & filter
   115→    enrich_with_impact(items, state.get("dimension_scores", {}))
   116→    items = [i for i in items if _passes_impact_floor(i)]
   117→
   118→    # 3. Plan-aware ordering
   119→    new_ids, skipped = _plan_presort(items, state, plan)
   120→    items.sort(key=item_sort_key)
   121→    _plan_postsort(items, skipped, plan, opts)
   122→
   123→    # 4. Finalize
   124→    if not items:
   125→        items += _empty_queue_fallback(plan)
   126→    total = len(items)
   127→    if opts.count is not None and opts.count > 0:
   128→        items = items[:opts.count]
   129→    if opts.explain:
   130→        for item in items:
   131→            item["explain"] = item_explain(item)
   132→
   133→    return {
   134→        "items": items,
   135→        "total": total,
   136→        "grouped": group_queue_items(items, "item"),
   137→        "new_ids": new_ids,
   138→    }
   139→
   140→
   141→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   142→# Pipeline helpers (private to this module)
   143→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   144→
   145→
   146→def _resolve_inputs(
   147→    opts: QueueBuildOptions, state: StateModel,
   148→) -> tuple[dict | None, str | None, str, float]:
   149→    """Resolve plan, scan_path, status, and subjective threshold from options."""
   150→    ctx = opts.context
   151→    plan = ctx.plan if ctx is not None else opts.plan
   152→
   153→    scan_path: str | None = (
   154→        state.get("scan_path")
   155→        if opts.scan_path is _SCAN_PATH_FROM_STATE
   156→        else opts.scan_path  # type: ignore[assignment]
   157→    )
   158→
   159→    status = opts.status
   160→    if status not in ALL_STATUSES:
   161→        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported status filter: {status}")
   162→
   163→    try:
   164→        threshold = float(opts.subjective_threshold)
   165→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   166→        threshold = 100.0
   167→    threshold = max(0.0, min(100.0, threshold))
   168→
   169→    return plan, scan_path, status, threshold
   170→
   171→
   172→def _gather_subjective_items(
   173→    state: StateModel,
   174→    opts: QueueBuildOptions,
   175→    plan: dict | None,
   176→    threshold: float,
   177→) -> list[dict]:
   178→    """Build synthetic subjective items, gated by SubjectiveVisibility policy."""
   179→    if not opts.include_subjective:
   180→        return []
   181→    if opts.status not in {"open", "all"}:
   182→        return []
   183→    if opts.chronic:
   184→        return []
   185→
   186→    ctx = opts.context
   187→    policy = (
   188→        (ctx.policy if ctx is not None else None)
   189→        or opts.policy
   190→        or compute_subjective_visibility(state, plan=plan)
   191→    )
   192→
   193→    candidates = build_subjective_items(
   194→        state, state.get("findings", {}), threshold=threshold,
   195→    )
   196→
   197→    # When a plan explicitly includes a subjective item in queue_order,
   198→    # surface it regardless of policy — the plan is authoritative.
   199→    plan_queue_set: set[str] = (
   200→        set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   201→        if plan
   202→        else set()
   203→    )
   204→
   205→    result: list[dict] = []
   206→    for item in candidates:
   207→        if not scope_matches(item, opts.scope):
   208→            continue
   209→        item_id = item.get("id", "")
   210→        if not policy.should_surface(item) and item_id not in plan_queue_set:
   211→            continue
   212→        result.append(item)
   213→    return result
   214→
   215→
   216→def _gather_workflow_items(
   217→    state: StateModel, plan: dict | None, status: str,
   218→) -> list[dict]:
   219→    """Inject triage stages, checkpoints, and create-plan when plan is active."""
   220→    if not plan or status not in {"open", "all"}:
   221→        return []
   222→
   223→    items: list[dict] = list(build_triage_stage_items(plan, state))
   224→    for builder in (
   225→        build_import_scores_item,
   226→        build_communicate_score_item,
   227→    ):
   228→        item = builder(plan, state)
   229→        if item is not None:
   230→            items.append(item)
   231→    plan_item = build_create_plan_item(plan)
   232→    if plan_item is not None:
   233→        items.append(plan_item)
   234→    return items
   235→
   236→
   237→_MIN_STANDALONE_IMPACT = 0.05
   238→
   239→
   240→def _passes_impact_floor(item: dict) -> bool:
   241→    """Return True if item should survive the impact floor filter."""
   242→    if item.get("kind") != "finding":
   243→        return True
   244→    if item.get("is_review") or item.get("is_subjective"):
   245→        return True
   246→    impact = item.get("estimated_impact")
   247→    return not impact or impact >= _MIN_STANDALONE_IMPACT
   248→
   249→
   250→def _plan_presort(
   251→    items: list[dict], state: StateModel, plan: dict | None,
   252→) -> tuple[set[str], list[dict]]:
   253→    """Enrich plan metadata and stamp sort keys before sorting.
   254→
   255→    Returns ``(new_ids, skipped)`` — skipped items are removed from
   256→    ``items`` in place and returned separately for post-sort re-append.
   257→    """
   258→    if not plan:
   259→        return set(), []
   260→
   261→    new_ids = _new_item_ids(state)
   262→    enrich_plan_metadata(items, plan)
   263→    stamp_plan_sort_keys(items, plan, new_ids)
   264→    remaining, skipped = separate_skipped(items, plan)
   265→    items[:] = remaining
   266→    return new_ids, skipped
   267→
   268→
   269→def _plan_postsort(
   270→    items: list[dict],
   271→    skipped: list[dict],
   272→    plan: dict | None,
   273→    opts: QueueBuildOptions,
   274→) -> None:
   275→    """Re-append skipped items, stamp positions, filter to cluster focus."""
   276→    if not plan:
   277→        return
   278→
   279→    if opts.include_skipped:
   280→        items.extend(skipped)
   281→    stamp_positions(items, plan)
   282→    focused = filter_cluster_focus(items, plan, opts.cluster)
   283→    items[:] = focused
   284→
   285→
   286→def _empty_queue_fallback(plan: dict | None) -> list[dict]:
   287→    """Return a 'run scan' nudge when an active plan cycle has cleared."""
   288→    if not plan:
   289→        return []
   290→    plan_scores = plan.get("plan_start_scores", {})
   291→    if plan_scores.get("strict") is None:
   292→        return []
   293→    return [{
   294→        "id": "workflow::run-scan",
   295→        "kind": "workflow_action",
   296→        "summary": "Queue cleared \u2014 run scan to finalize and reveal your updated score.",
   297→        "primary_command": "desloppify scan",
   298→        "file": "",
   299→        "detector": "workflow",
   300→        "confidence": "high",
   301→    }]
   302→
   303→
   304→__all__ = [
   305→    "ATTEST_EXAMPLE",
   306→    "QueueBuildOptions",
   307→    "QueueContext",
   308→    "WorkQueueResult",
   309→    "build_work_queue",
   310→    "collapse_clusters",
   311→    "group_queue_items",
   312→]
   313→
```

1→"""Predicates and small utilities for work queue items."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from fnmatch import fnmatch
     7→
     8→from desloppify.core.enums import finding_status_tokens
     9→from desloppify.core.registry import DETECTORS
    10→from desloppify.intelligence.integrity import (
    11→    is_holistic_subjective_finding,
    12→)
    13→
    14→ALL_STATUSES = set(finding_status_tokens(include_all=True))
    15→ACTION_TYPE_PRIORITY = {"auto_fix": 0, "refactor": 1, "manual_fix": 2, "reorganize": 3}
    16→ATTEST_EXAMPLE = (
    17→    "I have actually [DESCRIBE THE CONCRETE CHANGE YOU MADE] "
    18→    "and I am not gaming the score by resolving without fixing."
    19→)
    20→
    21→
    22→def detail_dict(item: dict) -> dict:
    23→    """Return finding detail as a dict; tolerate legacy/non-dict payloads."""
    24→    detail = item.get("detail")
    25→    return detail if isinstance(detail, dict) else {}
    26→
    27→
    28→def status_matches(item_status: str, status_filter: str) -> bool:
    29→    return status_filter == "all" or item_status == status_filter
    30→
    31→
    32→def is_subjective_finding(item: dict) -> bool:
    33→    detector = item.get("detector")
    34→    if detector in {"subjective_assessment"}:
    35→        return True
    36→    if detector == "holistic_review":
    37→        return True
    38→    return False
    39→
    40→
    41→def is_review_finding(item: dict) -> bool:
    42→    return item.get("detector") == "review"
    43→
    44→
    45→def is_subjective_queue_item(item: dict) -> bool:
    46→    """True for subjective work items, including collapsed subjective clusters."""
    47→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
    48→        return True
    49→    if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
    50→        members = item.get("members", [])
    51→        return bool(members) and all(
    52→            m.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension" for m in members
    53→        )
    54→    return False
    55→
    56→
    57→def review_finding_weight(item: dict) -> float:
    58→    """Return review issue weight aligned with issues list ordering."""
    59→    confidence = str(item.get("confidence", "low")).lower()
    60→    weight_by_confidence = {
    61→        "high": 1.0,
    62→        "medium": 0.7,
    63→        "low": 0.3,
    64→    }
    65→    weight = weight_by_confidence.get(confidence, 0.3)
    66→    if detail_dict(item).get("holistic"):
    67→        weight *= 10.0
    68→    return float(weight)
    69→
    70→
    71→def scope_matches(item: dict, scope: str | None) -> bool:
    72→    """Apply show-style pattern matching against a queue item."""
    73→    if not scope:
    74→        return True
    75→
    76→    item_id = item.get("id", "")
    77→    detector = item.get("detector", "")
    78→    filepath = item.get("file", "")
    79→    summary = item.get("summary", "")
    80→    dimension = detail_dict(item).get("dimension_name", "")
    81→    kind = item.get("kind", "")
    82→
    83→    if "*" in scope:
    84→        return any(
    85→            fnmatch(candidate, scope)
    86→            for candidate in (item_id, filepath, detector, dimension, summary)
    87→        )
    88→
    89→    if "::" in scope:
    90→        return item_id.startswith(scope)
    91→
    92→    lowered = scope.lower()
    93→    if kind == "subjective_dimension":
    94→        return (
    95→            lowered in item_id.lower()
    96→            or lowered in dimension.lower()
    97→            or lowered in summary.lower()
    98→        )
    99→
   100→    # Hash suffix: 8+ hex chars matches the tail segment of a finding ID.
   101→    if len(lowered) >= 8 and re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]+", lowered):
   102→        return item_id.lower().endswith("::" + lowered)
   103→
   104→    return (
   105→        detector == scope
   106→        or filepath == scope
   107→        or filepath.startswith(scope.rstrip("/") + "/")
   108→    )
   109→
   110→
   111→def slugify(text: str) -> str:
   112→    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9_]+", "_", text.lower()).strip("_")
   113→
   114→
   115→def supported_fixers_for_item(state: dict, item: dict) -> set[str] | None:
   116→    """Return supported fixers for an item's language when known."""
   117→    lang = str(item.get("lang", "") or "").strip()
   118→    if not lang:
   119→        return None
   120→
   121→    caps = state.get("lang_capabilities", {})
   122→    if not isinstance(caps, dict):
   123→        return None
   124→
   125→    lang_caps = caps.get(lang, {})
   126→    if not isinstance(lang_caps, dict):
   127→        return None
   128→
   129→    fixers = lang_caps.get("fixers")
   130→    if not isinstance(fixers, list):
   131→        return None
   132→    return {fixer for fixer in fixers if isinstance(fixer, str)}
   133→
   134→
   135→def primary_command_for_finding(
   136→    item: dict, *, supported_fixers: set[str] | None = None
   137→) -> str:
   138→    detector = item.get("detector", "")
   139→    meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   140→    if meta and meta.action_type == "auto_fix" and meta.fixers:
   141→        available_fixers = [
   142→            fixer
   143→            for fixer in meta.fixers
   144→            if supported_fixers is not None and fixer in supported_fixers
   145→        ]
   146→        if available_fixers:
   147→            return f"desloppify autofix {available_fixers[0]} --dry-run"
   148→    if detector == "subjective_review":
   149→        if is_holistic_subjective_finding(item):
   150→            return "desloppify review --prepare"
   151→        return "desloppify show subjective"
   152→    return f'desloppify plan resolve "{item.get("id", "")}" --note "<what you did>" --confirm'
   153→
   154→
   155→__all__ = [
   156→    "ALL_STATUSES",
   157→    "ATTEST_EXAMPLE",
   158→    "detail_dict",
   159→    "is_review_finding",
   160→    "is_subjective_finding",
   161→    "is_subjective_queue_item",
   162→    "primary_command_for_finding",
   163→    "review_finding_weight",
   164→    "scope_matches",
   165→    "slugify",
   166→    "status_matches",
   167→    "supported_fixers_for_item",
   168→]
   169→
